      b) 2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10
      c) Z, 0, 0, 0, Z, Z/2, 0, 0, Z ⊕ Z
      d) Z, Z/2, Z/2, 0, Z, 0, 0, 0, Z ⊕ Z
      e) 1, 2, 3, 5 (partitions of k)
      f) 1, 2, 3, 5, 7 (partitions of n)
      g) yes, no, yes, no, yes, yes, yes, no (χ = −1 odd)
      h) 1, 0, 0, 0, 1
      i) both: w₁⁴ = w₁²w₂ = w₁w₃ = 0, w₂² = w₄ = 1
      j) (25, 10); (18, 9)
      k) no (σ = 2); yes (it is cobordant to 2CP² = 0 in Ω^O)
      l) 0
      m) no: Pontryagin numbers (25, 10) ≠ (18, 9)
      n) K3 (σ = −16; spin 4-manifolds have σ ∈ 16Z)

M12. True (T) or false (F)? Give a counterexample for F.
     a) E^∞ of a convergent spectral sequence determines H_* up to
        isomorphism.
     b) The Serre SS needs π₁(B) to act trivially on H_*(F) (or local
        coefficients).
     c) χ(E) = χ(B)χ(F) for every fibration of finite complexes with
        simply connected base.
     d) H*(ΩS³; Z) is a polynomial ring.
     e) πᵢ(Sⁿ) is finite for all i ≠ n.
     f) The AHSS for K-theory collapses for every CW complex.
     g) K̃⁰(RP⁴) ≅ Z/2 ⊕ Z/2.
     h) ch : K(X) ⊗ Q → H^{ev}(X; Q) is an isomorphism for finite CW X.
     i) Every c ∈ H^{2n}(S^{2n}; Z) is cₙ of some vector bundle on S^{2n}.
     j) ψᵏ is a ring homomorphism.
     k) S⁶ admits an almost complex structure.
     l) S³¹ has 9 independent vector fields and no more.
     m) π₃^s = Z/24 is generated by ν, and η³ = 12ν.
     n) The Freudenthal map π₃(S²) → π₄(S³) is an isomorphism.
     o) If w(M) = 1 then M is parallelizable.
     p) A manifold with odd Euler characteristic does not bound.
     q) Every closed orientable 3-manifold bounds (oriented).
     r) Pontryagin classes of closed smooth manifolds are homotopy
        invariants.
     s) The signature σ(M⁴ᵏ) is an oriented cobordism invariant.
     t) Every closed smooth spin 4-manifold has σ ≡ 0 (mod 16).
     u) Sq^i is indecomposable in 𝒜 for every i ≥ 1.
     v) Ω^{fr}_* ≅ π_*^s.
     Answers:
       a) F: E^∞ = Z/2, Z/2 in total degree 2 fits Z/4 and (Z/2)²
       b) T          c) T (rationally, via Serre SS)
       d) F: divided power algebra, x² = 2γ₂
       e) F: π₇(S⁴) ⊇ Z (Hopf map ν)
       f) F: for K(Z, 3), d₃(ι₃) = Sq³_Z ι₃ ≠ 0 (it reduces to ι₃² ≠ 0)
       g) F: it is Z/4
       h) T          i) F: c₃ on S⁶ must be even
       j) T          k) T (via octonions)
       l) T (32 = 2⁵: ρ = 2 + 8 = 10)
       m) T          n) F: onto Z/2 with kernel 2Z
       o) F: RP¹⁵ has w = 1 but is not parallelizable
       p) T          q) T (Ω₃^{SO} = 0)
       r) F: smooth homotopy CP³'s have p₁ = (4 + 24j)x², j ∈ Z (they
          are topological invariants rationally, by Novikov, but not
          homotopy invariants)
       s) T          t) T (Rokhlin)
       u) F: Sq³ = Sq¹Sq²       v) T (Pontryagin-Thom)

TEACHING TIPS
  Struggling: draw every spectral sequence as a grid on squared paper
  first — mark the nonzero entries, then draw the arrows allowed by the
  bidegree and ask "what MUST die, given what I know about the total
  space?"; nine times out of ten the answer is forced (CPⁿ, ΩSⁿ, S³⟨3⟩,
  SU(3)). Keep a small table of standard values on the wall: stable
  stems up to 11, K and KO of a point, w(RPⁿ), c(CPⁿ), p(CPⁿ), p(HP²),
  L₁, L₂, Â₁, Td₂, and the low cobordism groups, and check each new
  computation against a known example (CP², K3, HP²). For characteristic
  classes, always reduce to line bundles (splitting principle) and
  expand power series with truncation at the top degree; for mod 2
  binomials use Lucas' theorem. Harder: prove the multiplicativity of
  the Serre SS and derive H*(K(Z, 3); Z) in degrees ≤ 8; compute
  K*(SU(3)/SO(3)) and exhibit a nonzero d₃ in the AHSS; prove Bott
  periodicity via the clutching argument of Atiyah-Bott; derive the
  vector-field number from Clifford modules and the J-homomorphism on
  real projective spaces; compute the Adams E₂-term through stem 13 by
  the May SS; and prove Thom's theorem Ω^O_* = Z/2[xᵢ : i ≠ 2ʲ − 1] by
  computing H*(MO) as a free 𝒜-module and reading off Milnor's
  generators of Ω^U_* via the Milnor genus sₙ[M] = ±1 or ±p.


================================================================================
TOPIC 13.5 — GEOMETRIC TOPOLOGY: LOW-DIMENSIONAL TOPOLOGY, KNOT THEORY,
3- AND 4-MANIFOLDS, FLOER THEORY
================================================================================
WHAT THE CHILD MUST LEARN
  - SURFACES: closed surfaces are classified by orientability and χ:
    Σ_g (χ = 2 − 2g) and N_k = #ᵏRP² (χ = 2 − k); with b boundary circles
    χ drops by b. MAPPING CLASS GROUP Mod(Σ) = Homeo⁺(Σ)/isotopy.
    DEHN TWIST T_a acts on H₁ by x ↦ x + (a·x)a (Picard-Lefschetz).
    T_a, T_b commute if a ∩ b = ∅ and satisfy the BRAID RELATION T_aT_bT_a
    = T_bT_aT_b if i(a, b) = 1. Mod(T²) = SL(2, Z); Mod(Σ_g) is generated
    by 2g + 1 twists (Humphries); H₁(Mod(Σ_g)) = Z/12, Z/10, 0 for g = 1,
    2, ≥ 3.
  - NIELSEN-THURSTON: every mapping class is PERIODIC, REDUCIBLE (fixes a
    multicurve) or PSEUDO-ANOSOV (stable/unstable measured foliations,
    stretch factor λ > 1). On T², A ∈ SL(2, Z): |tr A| < 2 or A = ±I
    periodic (orders 4, 6, 3 for tr 0, 1, −1); |tr A| = 2, A ≠ ±I
    reducible (± a twist power); |tr A| > 2 ANOSOV, λ = larger eigenvalue.
    The mapping torus T_A has H₁ = Z ⊕ coker(A − I), |coker| = |2 − tr A|
    when finite.
  - KNOTS AND LINKS: K ⊂ S³ up to ambient isotopy; diagrams are related by
    REIDEMEISTER MOVES R1, R2, R3. LINKING NUMBER lk = (sum of signs of
    crossings between the two components)/2 (Hopf ±1, Whitehead 0,
    T(2,4) ±2). FOX p-COLORINGS: 2·over ≡ under₁ + under₂ (mod p); their
    number is p·#Hom(H₁(Σ₂(K)), Z/p), so K is p-colourable (p prime)
    nontrivially iff p | det K. Trefoil: 9 three-colourings; unknot 3.
  - SEIFERT SURFACES: every oriented link bounds one (Seifert's algorithm;
    from a diagram with c crossings and s Seifert circles of a knot,
    g ≤ (c − s + 1)/2, equality for reduced ALTERNATING diagrams and for
    positive braids). SEIFERT MATRIX V_{ij} = lk(aᵢ, aⱼ⁺). ALEXANDER
    POLYNOMIAL Δ(t) = det(V − tVᵀ) up to ±tᵏ, symmetric, Δ(1) = ±1;
    2g(K) ≥ span Δ; DETERMINANT det K = |Δ(−1)| = |det(V + Vᵀ)| =
    |H₁(Σ₂(K))|; SIGNATURE σ(K) = sign(V + Vᵀ). Convention here: the
    right-handed (positive) trefoil has σ = −2.
  - CONWAY POLYNOMIAL: ∇(O) = 1, ∇(L₊) − ∇(L₋) = z∇(L₀), and Δ(t) =
    ∇(t^½ − t^(−½)). Values: 3₁: 1 + z², 4₁: 1 − z², 5₁: 1 + 3z² + z⁴,
    5₂: 1 + 2z², 6₁: 1 − 2z², Hopf link: ±z, split links: 0. A FIBERED
    knot (complement fibres over S¹ with fibre a Seifert surface) has
    monic Δ with deg = 2g; the monodromy on H₁(fibre) has characteristic
    polynomial Δ.
  - JONES POLYNOMIAL: KAUFFMAN BRACKET ⟨✕⟩ = A⟨≍⟩ + A⁻¹⟨)(⟩, ⟨D ⊔ O⟩ =
    (−A² − A⁻²)⟨D⟩, f(D) = (−A³)^(−w(D))⟨D⟩, V(t) = f(A = t^(−1/4)).
    SKEIN: t⁻¹V(L₊) − tV(L₋) = (t^½ − t^(−½))V(L₀). V(3₁ right) = t + t³ −
    t⁴, V(4₁) = t⁻² − t⁻¹ + 1 − t + t², V(mirror K)(t) = V(K)(t⁻¹);
    V(1) = 1, |V(−1)| = det K. KAUFFMAN-MURASUGI-THISTLETHWAITE: a reduced
    alternating diagram with c crossings has span V = c, so c is the
    crossing number (Tait conjecture).
  - KHOVANOV HOMOLOGY Kh^{h,q}(K): bigraded, Σ(−1)ʰ q^j rank Kh^{h,j} =
    (q + q⁻¹)V(K)|_{t = q²}; Kh(O) = Q₍₀,₁₎ ⊕ Q₍₀,₋₁₎, Kh(right trefoil)
    = Q at (h, q) = (0, 1), (0, 3), (2, 5), (3, 9). Detects the unknot
    (Kronheimer-Mrowka); Rasmussen s gives |s| ≤ 2g₄.
  - KNOT GROUPS: π₁(S³ ∖ K) via the WIRTINGER presentation (a generator
    per arc, a relation per crossing), H₁ = Z. Trefoil: ⟨x, y | xyx =
    yxy⟩ ≅ ⟨a, b | a² = b³⟩; TORUS KNOT T(p, q): ⟨a, b | aᵖ = b^q⟩, with
    genus (p − 1)(q − 1)/2, Δ = (t^{pq} − 1)(t − 1)/((tᵖ − 1)(t^q − 1)),
    T(p,q) = closure of (σ₁⋯σ_{p−1})^q. THURSTON: every knot is a torus
    knot, a SATELLITE (essential torus in the complement) or HYPERBOLIC;
    complements determine knots (Gordon-Luecke). The figure-eight
    complement is hyperbolic of volume 2v₃ = 2.02988..., v₃ = 1.01494...
  - 4-DIMENSIONAL KNOT INVARIANTS: K is SLICE if it bounds a smooth disc
    in B⁴; SLICE GENUS g₄(K) ≤ g(K), g₄ ≤ u(K) (unknotting number).
    Bounds: |σ|/2 ≤ g₄, |σ|/2 ≤ u. FOX-MILNOR: K slice ⇒ Δ(t) =
    f(t)f(t⁻¹), so det K is a square. K#(−K) is slice (square knot).
    MILNOR CONJECTURE (Kronheimer-Mrowka): g₄(T(p,q)) = u(T(p,q)) =
    (p − 1)(q − 1)/2.
  - 3-MANIFOLDS: every closed orientable M³ has a HEEGAARD SPLITTING
    (two handlebodies of genus g glued along Σ_g); Heegaard genus ≥ rank
    π₁ ≥ rank H₁. LENS SPACES L(p, q) (genus 1): π₁ = Z/p; L(p, q) ≅
    L(p, q′) iff q′ ≡ ±q^{±1} (mod p) (Reidemeister, Brody); homotopy
    equivalent iff qq′ ≡ ±n² (mod p) (Whitehead). L(7,1) ≃ L(7,2) but
    they are not homeomorphic. POINCARÉ SPHERE Σ(2,3,5): homology sphere
    with π₁ the binary icosahedral group of order 120.
  - DEHN SURGERY: S³_{p/q}(K) has H₁ = Z/|p|; S³_{p/q}(unknot) is a lens
    space L(p, q) (up to orientation convention), S³₀(O) = S² × S¹.
    LICKORISH-WALLACE: every closed orientable 3-manifold is integral
    surgery on a link in S³. For an integral framed link, H₁ = coker of
    the LINKING MATRIX. ±1 surgery on the right trefoil gives the
    Poincaré sphere and Σ(2,3,7) (up to orientation).
  - KIRBY CALCULUS: two framed links give the same 3-manifold iff related
    by BLOW-UPS (±1 unknots) and HANDLE SLIDES. BLOW-DOWN of a ∓1 unknot
    changes other framings by ±lk²; ROLFSEN TWIST (n twists about an
    unknot with coefficient r): r ↦ 1/(n + 1/r), rᵢ ↦ rᵢ + n·lk²;
    SLAM-DUNK: a meridian with coefficient r of an n-framed knot becomes
    n − 1/r. Linear plumbings with framings −a₁, ..., −a_k give L(p, q),
    p/q = a₁ − 1/(a₂ − 1/(⋯ − 1/a_k)).
  - GEOMETRIZATION (Thurston, Perelman): M³ closed orientable = prime
    pieces (Kneser-Milnor), cut along JSJ tori into Seifert fibred and
    hyperbolic pieces. EIGHT GEOMETRIES: S³, E³, H³, S² × R, H² × R,
    Nil, SL̃₂(R), Sol. Seifert fibred: base orbifold χ and Euler number
    e decide (χ > 0: S³ / S² × R; χ = 0: Nil / E³; χ < 0: SL̃₂ / H² × R
    for e ≠ 0 / e = 0). Torus bundles: periodic E³, reducible Nil, Anosov
    Sol. POINCARÉ CONJECTURE: π₁ = 1 ⇒ M ≅ S³. MOSTOW RIGIDITY: volume
    is a topological invariant of finite-volume hyperbolic 3-manifolds.
    THURSTON HYPERBOLIC DEHN SURGERY: all but finitely many fillings of a
    hyperbolic knot are hyperbolic; figure-eight: exceptional slopes ∞,
    0, ±1, ±2, ±3, ±4 (10, the maximum possible).
  - 4-MANIFOLDS: INTERSECTION FORM Q_X on H₂(X)/tors, symmetric,
    UNIMODULAR (Poincaré duality); invariants rank b₂, signature σ =
    b⁺ − b⁻, type (even iff w₂ = 0 for π₁ = 1). Indefinite forms: odd ⇒
    a⟨1⟩ ⊕ b⟨−1⟩, even ⇒ aE₈ ⊕ bH (σ ≡ 0 mod 8). Examples: CP² ⟨1⟩,
    S² × S² H, CP² # CP̄² ⟨1⟩ ⊕ ⟨−1⟩, K3 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H (b₂ = 22, σ = −16,
    χ = 24). For π₁ = 1: χ = 2 + b₂. HIRZEBRUCH σ = p₁/3.
  - BIG THEOREMS IN DIMENSION 4: FREEDMAN: closed simply connected
    TOPOLOGICAL 4-manifolds ↔ forms (plus Kirby-Siebenmann for odd forms);
    every form occurs (E₈-manifold). ROKHLIN: smooth spin ⇒ σ ≡ 0 mod 16.
    DONALDSON: smooth definite ⇒ diagonal (±n⟨1⟩). FURUTA (10/8): smooth
    spin with σ ≠ 0 ⇒ b₂ ≥ (10/8)|σ| + 2. Consequence: exotic R⁴s.
  - SEIBERG-WITTEN: for b⁺ > 1, SW_X : Spin^c(X) → Z, a diffeomorphism
    invariant; BASIC classes K. Kähler (b⁺ > 1) ⇒ SW ≠ 0; X₁ # X₂ with
    b⁺(Xᵢ) ≥ 1 ⇒ SW = 0; positive scalar curvature ⇒ SW = 0. ADJUNCTION
    INEQUALITY: Σ ⊂ X embedded, g ≥ 1, [Σ]² ≥ 0 ⇒ 2g − 2 ≥ [Σ]² + |K·Σ|.
    THOM CONJECTURE: a degree-d curve in CP² minimises genus in its class,
    g = (d − 1)(d − 2)/2. Degree-d surfaces X_d ⊂ CP³: e = d(d² − 4d + 6),
    σ = −d(d² − 4)/3; X₅ is homeomorphic to 9CP² # 44CP̄² but not
    diffeomorphic.
  - HEEGAARD FLOER HOMOLOGY: from a pointed Heegaard diagram, CF̂ is
    generated by intersection points of Tα ∩ Tβ in Sym^g(Σ), differential
    counts holomorphic discs. For a rational homology sphere Y, χ(HF̂(Y))
    = |H₁(Y)| so rank HF̂ ≥ |H₁|; equality: L-SPACE (S³, lens spaces,
    Σ(2,3,5), all elliptic manifolds). HF̂(Y₁ # Y₂) = HF̂(Y₁) ⊗ HF̂(Y₂).
    d-INVARIANT d(Y) ∈ Q, a rational homology cobordism invariant;
    d(Σ(2,3,5)) = 2 (oriented as ∂ of the negative E₈ plumbing).
  - KNOT FLOER HOMOLOGY HFK̂(K) = ⊕ HFK̂_m(K, a): graded Euler
    characteristic Σ(−1)^m rank HFK̂_m(K, a) tᵃ = Δ_K(t). DETECTS GENUS:
    g(K) = max{a : HFK̂(K, a) ≠ 0}; DETECTS FIBEREDNESS: rank HFK̂(K, g)
    = 1 iff fibered (Ghiggini, Ni). Alternating knots: HFK̂ is supported
    on m = a + σ/2, rank = det. τ(K): |τ| ≤ g₄, τ(T(p,q)) = (p−1)(q−1)/2,
    τ additive, τ(mirror) = −τ. L-SPACE KNOTS (torus knots): Δ has
    coefficients ±1 alternating in sign; S³_p(K) is an L-space for p ≥
    2g − 1.
  - OTHER FLOER/GAUGE THEORIES: Floer's INSTANTON homology I_*(Y) of a
    homology sphere with χ = 2λ(Y), CASSON INVARIANT λ(S³_{1/n}(K)) =
    (n/2)Δ″_K(1); λ mod 2 = ROKHLIN invariant μ(Y) = σ(W)/8 mod 2 for a
    spin W with ∂W = Y. MONOPOLE Floer HM ≅ HF ≅ ECH. Σ(2,3,5) has μ = 1
    and d ≠ 0, so it bounds no homology ball.

PART A — DESCRIPTIVE
--------------------
D1. (a) Show that a Dehn twist T_a on a surface acts on H₁ by T_a(x) = x
    + (a·x)a, and write the matrices of T_a, T_b on H₁(T²) = Z² for the
    curves a = (1, 0), b = (0, 1). (b) Verify T_aT_bT_a = T_bT_aT_b and
    (T_aT_b)⁶ = I in SL(2, Z). (c) Classify A = T_aT_b⁻¹ and B = T_aT_b by
    Nielsen-Thurston, give the stretch factor of the Anosov one, and
    compute H₁ of the two mapping tori and their geometries.
    Answer: (a) T_a = [[1,1],[0,1]], T_b = [[1,0],[−1,1]]. (b) both sides
    equal [[0,1],[−1,0]], (T_aT_b)³ = −I. (c) A = [[2,1],[1,1]] Anosov,
    λ = (3 + √5)/2, H₁(T_A) = Z, geometry Sol; B = [[0,1],[−1,1]] periodic
    of order 6, H₁(T_B) = Z, geometry E³ (flat).
    Teacher explains: (a) T_a is the identity outside an annulus around
    a. A curve x crossing a transversally once is cut and re-glued after
    going once around a, so its class changes by ±a at each crossing; with
    signs, T_a(x) = x + (a·x)a (for the left-handed twist and a·b = 1).
    For a = (1, 0), b = (0, 1): T_a fixes a and sends b ↦ b + a, so T_a
    = [[1,1],[0,1]] (columns are images). T_b fixes b and sends a ↦ a +
    (b·a)b = a − b, so T_b = [[1,0],[−1,1]]. (b) T_aT_b = [[0,1],[−1,1]],
    then T_aT_bT_a = [[0,1],[−1,0]]. T_bT_a = [[1,1],[−1,0]], T_bT_aT_b =
    [[0,1],[−1,0]]: equal. Put S = [[0,1],[−1,0]] = T_aT_bT_a; then
    (T_aT_b)³ = (T_aT_bT_a)(T_bT_aT_b) = S² = −I, so (T_aT_b)⁶ = I.
    (c) T_b⁻¹ = [[1,0],[1,1]], A = T_aT_b⁻¹ = [[2,1],[1,1]]: tr 3 > 2,
    ANOSOV. Eigenvalues solve λ² − 3λ + 1 = 0: λ = (3 ± √5)/2, stretch
    factor (3 + √5)/2 ≈ 2.618 = φ². B = T_aT_b has tr 1: its
    characteristic polynomial λ² − λ + 1 divides λ⁶ − 1 and B has order
    6: PERIODIC. H₁(T_A) = Z ⊕ coker(A − I); A − I = [[1,1],[1,0]] has
    det −1, so the coker is 0 and H₁ = Z; likewise det(B − I) =
    det[[−1,1],[−1,0]] = 1 gives H₁(T_B) = Z (|2 − tr| = 1 both times).
    T_A is a Sol manifold; T_B, the mapping torus of a periodic map of T²,
    is finitely covered by T³ and is FLAT (E³). These are exactly the
    monodromies of the figure-eight knot (char. poly t² − 3t + 1 =
    −tΔ_{4₁}) and of the trefoil (t² − t + 1): the fibre is a punctured
    torus and the knot complements are hyperbolic and Seifert fibred.
    Common mistakes: confusing |tr| = 2 (reducible, Nil) with −I (tr −2,
    periodic); composing matrices in the wrong order (the first twist
    applied is on the right); forgetting λ⁻¹ is the other eigenvalue.
    Now you try: classify [[3,2],[4,3]] and [[−1,1],[−1,0]] and give
    |tors H₁| of their mapping tori. (Anosov, λ = 3 + 2√2, |2 − 6| = 4;
    periodic of order 3, |2 − (−1)| = 3)

D2. (a) Prove that the number of Fox p-colourings of a diagram is a knot
    invariant. (b) Count the 3-colourings of the standard trefoil and
    figure-eight diagrams, and the 5-colourings of the figure-eight;
    conclude that 3₁ ≠ O, 4₁ ≠ O, 3₁ ≠ 4₁. (c) Compute the linking numbers
    of the Hopf link, T(2, 4) and the Whitehead link, and use 3-colourings
    to show the Whitehead link is not the 2-component unlink.
    Answer: (b) 9, 3, 25. (c) ±1, ±2, 0; Whitehead has 3 colourings, the
    unlink 9.
    Teacher explains: (a) Check the three Reidemeister moves locally,
    with the colours outside the disc fixed. R1: the kink has one crossing
    with over = under₁ = x so under₂ = 2x − x = x: colourings match. R2:
    two crossings with over arc x and incoming y: the middle arc is
    forced to be 2x − y and the outgoing arc 2x − (2x − y) = y: a
    bijection. R3: top strand x over middle y and bottom z, y over z. One
    side: z ↦ 2y − z, then x acts: 2x − y and 2x − 2y + z. Other side: x acts
    first, 2x − y and 2x − z, then 2(2x − y) − (2x − z) = 2x − 2y + z. Same
    outgoing colours, and the interior arc is determined, so colourings
    correspond bijectively. (b) Trefoil arcs a, b, c; each crossing gives 2a ≡
    b + c, etc. Mod 3 every relation reads a + b + c ≡ 0: two free choices, 3²
    = 9 colourings (6 nontrivial). Figure-eight, arcs A, B, C, D:
       2A = B + C,  2C = D + A,  2B = C + D,  2D = A + B.
    The coloring matrix has a 3 × 3 minor of determinant ±5 = det 4₁. Mod
    3 it has rank 3, only the 3 constant colourings; mod 5 rank 2, 5² = 25
    colourings. The unknot has exactly p colourings, so 3₁ and 4₁ are
    knotted, and 3₁ ≠ 4₁ (9 ≠ 3). (c) lk = ½ Σ signs over crossings
    between components: Hopf link has 2 crossings of the same sign: ±1;
    T(2,4) has 4: ±2; the Whitehead link's clasp has two crossings of
    opposite sign between the components (the others are self-crossings):
    0. Its determinant is 16, so H₁(Σ₂) has order 16, prime to 3, and
    #3-colourings = 3·#Hom(H₁(Σ₂), Z/3) = 3; the unlink has 3² = 9.
    Common mistakes: counting self-crossings in lk; forgetting the
    constant colourings; concluding "lk = 0 ⇒ split".
    Now you try: how many 3-colourings do 6₁ and the granny knot 3₁ # 3₁
    have? (9 and 27: H₁(Σ₂) = Z/9 and Z/3 ⊕ Z/3)

D3. (a) Explain Seifert's algorithm and prove g(T(p, q)) ≤ (p − 1)(q −
    1)/2 from the braid (σ₁⋯σ_{p−1})^q. (b) For the right trefoil with
    Seifert matrix V = [[−1,1],[0,−1]] and the figure-eight with V =
    [[1,1],[0,−1]] compute Δ, det and σ. (c) Which of 3₁, 4₁, 5₂ (Δ = 2t
    − 3 + 2t⁻¹) can be fibered, and what is the genus of each?
    Answer: (a) g ≤ ((p−1)q − p + 1)/2. (b) 3₁: t − 1 + t⁻¹, 3, −2; 4₁:
    −t + 3 − t⁻¹, 5, 0. (c) 3₁, 4₁ fibered, 5₂ not; all genus 1.
    Teacher explains: (a) Orient the diagram, smooth every crossing in the
    orientation-respecting way: we get s disjoint SEIFERT CIRCLES. Nested
    circles are pushed to different heights, each bounds a disc, and at
    each crossing a half-twisted band joins two discs. The result is a
    connected orientable surface F with χ(F) = s − c, so for a knot 2g(F)
    = 1 − χ = c − s + 1. The closure of a p-strand braid has s = p
    circles (one per strand); (σ₁⋯σ_{p−1})^q has c = (p − 1)q crossings,
    so 2g ≤ (p − 1)q − p + 1 = (p − 1)(q − 1). (Equality holds, e.g.
    because deg Δ_{T(p,q)} = (p − 1)(q − 1).) (b) Trefoil:
       V − tVᵀ = [[−1 + t, 1], [−t, −1 + t]],  det = (t − 1)² + t
    = t² − t + 1 ≐ t − 1 + t⁻¹. V + Vᵀ = [[−2,1],[1,−2]]: det 3, trace
    −4 < 0, negative definite, σ = −2. Figure-eight: V − tVᵀ =
    [[1 − t, 1], [−t, t − 1]], det = −(1 − t)² + t = −t² + 3t − 1 ≐ −t +
    3 − t⁻¹; V + Vᵀ = [[2,1],[1,−2]] has det −5 < 0: indefinite, σ = 0,
    det 4₁ = 5. Check: Δ(−1) = −3 and 5 in absolute value. (c) The genus
    bound 2g ≥ span Δ = 2 and the explicit genus-1 surfaces give g = 1
    for all three. A fibered knot has monic Δ (the monodromy is an
    automorphism of H₁(F) = Z^{2g} with characteristic polynomial Δ, so
    the leading coefficient is det = ±1). 5₂ has leading coefficient 2:
    NOT fibered. 3₁ and 4₁ are fibered (D1: monodromies of tr 1 and 3).
    Common mistakes: using V − Vᵀ (the intersection form, det 1) instead
    of V + Vᵀ; normalising Δ with the wrong unit; believing monic Δ ⇒
    fibered (true for alternating knots, false in general).
    Now you try: V = [[1,1],[0,−2]]: compute Δ, det, σ; which knot?
    (−2t + 5 − 2t⁻¹, 9, 0: the stevedore knot 6₁)

D4. (a) Compute the Kauffman bracket of the Hopf link diagram and use the
    skein relation to compute V of the positive Hopf link and of the
    right trefoil. (b) Deduce that the trefoil is CHIRAL and that the
    figure-eight polynomial is consistent with amphichirality. (c) Use
    span V to find the crossing numbers of 3₁, 4₁, 5₂ (V = t − t² + 2t³ −
    t⁴ + t⁵ − t⁶) and check (q + q⁻¹)V(q²) against Kh(3₁).
    Answer: (a) ⟨Hopf⟩ = −A⁴ − A⁻⁴; V(Hopf₊) = −t^½ − t^{5/2}; V(3₁) = t
    + t³ − t⁴. (b) V(3₁)(t⁻¹) ≠ V(3₁)(t). (c) 3, 4, 5; q + q³ + q⁵ − q⁹.
    Teacher explains: (a) The Hopf diagram has 2 crossings, 4 states.
    The two "pure" states (both A, both A⁻¹) give 2 circles, the two mixed
    states 1 circle. Each circle beyond the first costs d = −A² − A⁻²:
    ⟨Hopf⟩ = A²d + 2 + A⁻²d = −A⁴ − 1 + 2 − 1 − A⁻⁴ = −A⁴ − A⁻⁴.
    For the skein method: L₊ = Hopf₊, L₋ = 2-component unlink (V = −t^½
    − t^(−½)), L₀ = unknot:
       t⁻¹V₊ = t(−t^½ − t^(−½)) + (t^½ − t^(−½)) = −t^{3/2} − t^(−½),
    V₊ = −t^{5/2} − t^½. For the right trefoil at one crossing: L₊ = 3₁,
    L₋ = unknot, L₀ = Hopf₊:
       t⁻¹V(3₁) = t + (t^½ − t^(−½))(−t^{5/2} − t^½) = 1 + t² − t³,
    so V(3₁) = t + t³ − t⁴. (b) The mirror has V(t⁻¹) = t⁻¹ + t⁻³ − t⁻⁴ ≠
    V(t), so 3₁ ≇ its mirror. V(4₁) = t⁻² − t⁻¹ + 1 − t + t² is
    symmetric, as it must be (4₁ is amphichiral). (c) The standard
    diagrams are reduced alternating, so crossing number = span V: 3₁: 4 −
    1 = 3; 4₁: 2 − (−2) = 4; 5₂: 6 − 1 = 5. Checks: V(1) = 1 and |V(−1)| =
    det: 5₂: −1 − 1 − 2 − 1 − 1 − 1 = −7. Khovanov: (q + q⁻¹)(q² + q⁶ −
    q⁸) = q + q³ + q⁵ + q⁷ − q⁷ − q⁹ = q + q³ + q⁵ − q⁹, and Σ(−1)ʰqʲ over
    (0,1), (0,3), (2,5), (3,9) gives q + q³ + q⁵ − q⁹. ✓
    Common mistakes: forgetting the writhe normalisation (−A³)^(−w);
    substituting A = t^{1/4} instead of t^(−1/4) (gives the mirror);
    using span V for non-alternating diagrams (only span V ≤ c holds).
    Now you try: V(5₁) = t² + t⁴ − t⁵ + t⁶ − t⁷: check V(1), |V(−1)|,
    span. (1; 5 = det; 5 = crossing number)

D5. (a) Derive the Wirtinger presentation of the trefoil group and show
    it is ≅ ⟨a, b | a² = b³⟩; find a surjection onto S₃. (b) Show that
    this group is non-abelian, so the trefoil is knotted, and that the
    centre ⟨a²⟩ recovers {p, q} for T(p, q). (c) Compute Δ, genus and
    det of T(2,5), T(3,4), T(3,5) and decide which pairs among T(2,5),
    T(3,4), 5₁, 4₁ could be equal.
    Answer: (a) ⟨x, y | xyx = yxy⟩, a = xyx, b = xy; x ↦ (12), y ↦ (23).
    (b) G/⟨a²⟩ = Z/2 * Z/3. (c) T(2,5): t⁴ − t³ + t² − t + 1, g 2, det
    5; T(3,4): t⁶ − t⁵ + t³ − t + 1, g 3, det 3; T(3,5): t⁸ − t⁷ + t⁵ −
    t⁴ + t³ − t + 1, g 4, det 1. T(2,5) = 5₁; all other pairs distinct.
    Teacher explains: (a) Three arcs x, y, z; at each crossing the over
    arc conjugates: z = xyx⁻¹, x = yzy⁻¹, y = zxz⁻¹ (one is redundant).
    Substituting z: x = y·xyx⁻¹·y⁻¹, i.e. xyx = yxy. Put a = xyx, b = xy.
    Then b³ = xyxyxy = (xyx)(yxy) = (xyx)(xyx) = a². Conversely b⁻¹a =
    y⁻¹x⁻¹xyx = x and a⁻¹b² = x⁻¹y⁻¹x⁻¹xyxy = y, so the maps are
    inverse isomorphisms. The map x ↦ (12), y ↦ (23) respects xyx = yxy
    (both are (13)); its image contains two transpositions, so it is
    onto S₃. Such maps sending meridians to transpositions are exactly the
    nontrivial 3-colourings (6 of them). (b) S₃ is non-abelian, so G is; the
    unknot group is Z. In ⟨a, b | aᵖ = b^q⟩, aᵖ is central, and the quotient
    is Z/p * Z/q, which has trivial centre: so Z(G) = ⟨aᵖ⟩ and G/Z(G) = Z/p *
    Z/q determines {p, q} (via torsion orders). (c) Using Δ =
    (t^{pq} − 1)(t − 1)/((tᵖ − 1)(t^q − 1)):
      T(2,5): (t¹⁰ − 1)(t − 1)/((t² − 1)(t⁵ − 1)) = (t⁵ + 1)/(t + 1)
             = t⁴ − t³ + t² − t + 1, Δ(−1) = 5,
      T(3,4): t⁶ − t⁵ + t³ − t + 1, Δ(−1) = 1 + 1 − 1 + 1 + 1 = 3,
      T(3,5): t⁸ − t⁷ + t⁵ − t⁴ + t³ − t + 1, Δ(−1) = 1.
    Genera (p − 1)(q − 1)/2 = 2, 3, 4. T(2,5) is the 5-crossing torus
    knot 5₁. 4₁ has genus 1, the others ≥ 2; T(3,4) has genus 3 ≠ 2.
    Common mistakes: forgetting the redundant Wirtinger relation; the
    identity Δ(1) = 1 is a check, not a theorem about T(p, q) only;
    confusing torus knots with torus LINKS (gcd(p, q) ≠ 1).
    Now you try: Δ and g of T(2,7) and T(4,5). (t⁶ − t⁵ + ⋯ + 1 all
    signs alternating, g 3; t¹² − t¹¹ + t⁸ − t⁶ + t⁴ − t + 1, g 6)

D6. (a) Describe the genus-1 Heegaard splitting of L(p, q), compute π₁
    and H_* and explain why χ(HF̂) forces rank HF̂ ≥ p. (b) Decide which
    of L(5,1), L(5,2), L(5,3), L(5,4) are homeomorphic or homotopy
    equivalent. (c) Show L(7,1) and L(7,2) are homotopy equivalent but
    not homeomorphic.
    Answer: (a) π₁ = Z/p, H₁ = Z/p, H₂ = 0, H₃ = Z. (b) L(5,1) ≅ L(5,4),
    L(5,2) ≅ L(5,3), and L(5,1) ≄ L(5,2) (not even homotopy equivalent).
    (c) 1·2 ≡ 3² (mod 7) but 2 ≢ ±1.
    Teacher explains: (a) Glue two solid tori V₁ ∪ V₂ so that the
    meridian of V₂ goes to a curve on ∂V₁ running p times along the core
    (longitude) and q times around the meridian. Van Kampen: π₁(V₁) = Z
    generated by the core, and gluing the meridian disc of V₂ kills the
    class of the curve, which is p times the generator: π₁ = Z/p. The
    manifold is closed orientable, so H₃ = Z, H₁ = Z/p, H₂ ≅ H¹ =
    Hom(Z/p, Z) = 0. The standard genus-1 Heegaard diagram (α, β on T²
    meeting in p points) gives CF̂ of rank p with no differential, so
    HF̂ has rank p — an L-space; in general χ(HF̂(Y)) = |H₁(Y)|, so the
    rank is at least p for every Y with H₁ = Z/p. (b) Homeomorphism: q′
    ≡ ±q^{±1} (mod 5). Classes: {1, 4} (±1) and {2, 3} (2⁻¹ = 3). Homotopy
    equivalence needs qq′ ≡ ±n²: squares mod 5 are 1, 4 so ±n² ∈ {1, 4};
    for q = 1, q′ = 2 we get 2 ∉ {1, 4}: not homotopy equivalent. (c)
    Squares mod 7: 1, 2, 4; with sign: {1, 2, 4, 3, 5, 6} — every unit.
    So 1·2 = 2 ≡ 3² and L(7,1) ≃ L(7,2). Homeomorphism needs 2 ≡ ±1 or ±1⁻¹
    = ±1 (mod 7): false. The distinguishing invariant is Reidemeister
    torsion (or the d-invariants of HF⁺).
    Common mistakes: using "q′ ≡ ±q" only and forgetting inverses; using
    the homotopy criterion for homeomorphism; mixing up H₂ = 0 with
    torsion in H² (H² = Z/p).
    Now you try: are L(12,5) and L(12,7) homeomorphic? L(11,2), L(11,6)?
    (yes: 7 ≡ −5; yes: 6 = 2⁻¹ mod 11)

D7. (a) Show H₁(S³_{p/q}(K)) = Z/|p| for every knot K, and that integral
    surgery on a framed link L = K₁ ∪ ⋯ ∪ Kₙ has H₁ = coker Λ, Λ the
    linking matrix (Λᵢᵢ = framing). (b) Compute H₁ for: the Hopf link
    with framings (2, 2) and (0, n); the Borromean rings with framings
    (0, 0, 0); the linear chain (−3, −2, −2); the E₈ plumbing (all −2).
    (c) Use a slam-dunk and continued fractions to identify the chains
    (−3, −2) and (−3, −2, −2) as lens spaces.
    Answer: (a) proof below. (b) Z/3; 0 (S³ for all n); Z³ (T³); Z/7; 0
    (the Poincaré sphere). (c) L(5,2) and L(7,3).
    Teacher explains: (a) Let E = S³ ∖ νK. By Alexander duality H₁(E) =
    Z generated by the meridian μ, and the longitude λ is null-homologous
    in E (it bounds a Seifert surface). Gluing a solid torus so that its
    meridian goes to pμ + qλ adds one 2-cell along that curve and one
    3-cell, so H₁ = Z/⟨pμ + qλ⟩ = Z/⟨pμ⟩ = Z/|p|. For a framed link, the
    complement has H₁ = Zⁿ on the meridians μ₁, ..., μₙ, and the framed
    curve on ∂νKᵢ is homologous to Σⱼ lk(Kᵢ, Kⱼ)μⱼ with lk(Kᵢ, Kᵢ) :=
    framing. Killing these n classes gives H₁ = Zⁿ/ΛZⁿ = coker Λ; it is
    finite iff det Λ ≠ 0, of order |det Λ|.
    (b) Hopf (2, 2): Λ = [[2,1],[1,2]], det 3: Z/3. Hopf (0, n): Λ =
    [[0,1],[1,n]], det −1: H₁ = 0; indeed, slam-dunking the n-framed
    meridian into the 0-framed unknot leaves the unknot with coefficient 0 −
    1/n = −1/n, and S³_{−1/n}(O) = S³. Borromean rings: pairwise lk = 0, Λ =
    0, H₁ = Z³ (the manifold is T³). Chain (−3, −2, −2): Λ =
    [[−3,1,0],[1,−2,1],[0,1,−2]], det = −3(4 − 1) − 1·(−2) = −7: Z/7 (cyclic,
    since the chain gives a lens space). E₈: det = 1: a homology sphere, the
    Poincaré sphere Σ(2,3,5).
    (c) Chain (−3, −2): K₂ is a meridian of K₁; slam-dunk K₂ into K₁:
    K₁ becomes the unknot with coefficient −3 − 1/(−2) = −5/2, and
    S³_{−5/2}(O) = L(5,2) (convention S³_{−p/q}(O) = L(p, q)). Chain (−3,
    −2, −2): first −2 − 1/(−2) = −3/2, then −3 − 1/(−3/2) = −3 + 2/3 =
    −7/3: L(7,3). In general p/q = [a₁, ..., a_k]⁻ = a₁ − 1/(a₂ − ⋯):
    7/3 = 3 − 1/(3/2), 3/2 = 2 − 1/2. ✓
    Common mistakes: forgetting that the framing enters the diagonal of
    Λ; writing Z/p ⊕ Z/q for a chain (it is cyclic of order |det|);
    orientation: S³_{p/q}(O) = −L(p, q) in this convention.
    Now you try: identify (−2, −2, −2, −2) and (−5) and give |H₁| of the
    Hopf link with framings (3, −4). (L(5,4) ≅ L(5,1); L(5,1); 13)

D8. (a) State the geometrization theorem and list the eight geometries.
    (b) Give the geometry of: T³, S² × S¹, Σ₂ × S¹, L(p, q), the Poincaré
    sphere, Σ(2,3,7), the Heisenberg manifold (torus bundle with monodromy
    [[1,1],[0,1]]), the torus bundle with monodromy [[2,1],[1,1]], and
    the figure-eight complement. (c) Explain why the figure-eight
    complement has volume 2v₃ ≈ 2.0299, deduce it is not homeomorphic to
    the 5₂ complement (volume ≈ 2.8281), and list its exceptional slopes.
    Answer: (b) E³, S² × R, H² × R, S³, S³, SL̃₂, Nil, Sol, H³. (c) two
    regular ideal tetrahedra; volumes differ; ∞, 0, ±1, ±2, ±3, ±4.
    Teacher explains: (a) A closed orientable 3-manifold splits uniquely
    as a connected sum of primes (Kneser-Milnor); each prime is cut along
    a minimal family of incompressible tori (JSJ) into pieces that are
    Seifert fibred or hyperbolic (Thurston, Perelman). Geometries: S³, E³,
    H³, S² × R, H² × R, Nil, SL̃₂(R), Sol. (b) T³ = R³/Z³: E³. S² × S¹:
    S² × R. Σ₂ × S¹: H² × R (base χ = −2, e = 0). Lens spaces and Σ(2,3,5)
    = S³/(binary icosahedral) have finite π₁: S³. Σ(2,3,7) is Seifert
    with base S²(2, 3, 7): χ_orb = 1/2 + 1/3 + 1/7 − 1 = −1/42 < 0 and e ≠
    0 (a homology sphere): SL̃₂. For Σ(2,3,5), χ_orb = 1/30 > 0: S³, as
    said. Torus bundles: [[1,1],[0,1]] has tr 2 (reducible): Nil;
    [[2,1],[1,1]] has tr 3 (Anosov): Sol. The figure-eight complement:
    H³ (Thurston). (c) Glue two regular ideal tetrahedra (dihedral angles
    π/3, each of volume v₃ = 1.0149416...) along faces as in the knot
    picture: at the edges six angles π/3 meet, total 2π, so the structure
    is complete hyperbolic; vol = 2v₃ = 2.0298832.... MOSTOW rigidity: the
    hyperbolic structure, hence the volume, is determined by π₁, so a
    complement of volume 2.8281... (5₂) is not homeomorphic. Thurston's
    Dehn surgery theorem leaves finitely many non-hyperbolic fillings;
    for 4₁ they are ∞ (S³), 0 (the Sol torus bundle with monodromy
    [[2,1],[1,1]], since 4₁ is fibered of genus 1), ±1 (±Σ(2,3,7)), ±2,
    ±3 (small Seifert fibred) and ±4 (containing an incompressible
    torus): 10 slopes, the maximum (Lackenby-Meyerhoff).
    Common mistakes: calling all Seifert manifolds "H² × R"; forgetting
    that tr = −2 with A = −I is periodic (E³); thinking volume is only a
    geometric, not a topological, invariant.
    Now you try: geometry of the unit tangent bundle of Σ₂, of RP³ #
    RP³ and of the torus bundle with A = [[0,−1],[1,0]]. (SL̃₂; S² × R;
    E³)

D9. (a) Explain why the intersection form Q_X of a closed oriented
    4-manifold is unimodular, and state the classification of indefinite
    unimodular forms. (b) For degree-d surfaces X_d ⊂ CP³ we have e(X_d) =
    d(d² − 4d + 6), σ = −d(d² − 4)/3, π₁ = 1, and X_d is spin iff d is
    even. Find b₂, b±, type and the form for d = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5. (c) Show
    that (S² × S²) # CP² and CP² # CP² # CP̄² have isomorphic forms, but
    S² × S² and CP² # CP̄² do not.
    Answer: (b) d = 1: ⟨1⟩; d = 2: H; d = 3: ⟨1⟩ ⊕ 6⟨−1⟩; d = 4 (K3): b₂
    22, σ −16, 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H; d = 5: b⁺ 9, b⁻ 44, 9⟨1⟩ ⊕ 44⟨−1⟩. (c) both
    odd, rank 3, σ 1; H is even, ⟨1⟩ ⊕ ⟨−1⟩ odd.
    Teacher explains: (a) Q(a, b) = ⟨PD(a) ∪ PD(b), [X]⟩ on H₂/tors. By
    Poincaré duality and universal coefficients, H₂/tors → Hom(H₂/tors,
    Z), a ↦ Q(a, ·), is an isomorphism, i.e. the Gram matrix has det ±1.
    Invariants: rank, signature, type (even: Q(a, a) always even). An
    INDEFINITE unimodular form is determined by these: odd ⇒ a⟨1⟩ ⊕
    b⟨−1⟩; even ⇒ σ ≡ 0 (mod 8) and Q ≅ (σ/8)E₈ ⊕ bH (E₈ positive definite
    rank 8, H = [[0,1],[1,0]]). Definite forms are NOT classified by these
    invariants (E₈ ⊕ E₈ ≇ D₁₆⁺, both even rank 16). (b) With π₁ = 1, b₁ =
    b₃ = 0 so b₂ = e − 2, b± = (b₂ ± σ)/2:
       d = 1: e 3, σ 1, b₂ 1, (1, 0): CP², ⟨1⟩.
       d = 2: e 4, σ 0, b₂ 2, (1, 1), even: H (the quadric S² × S²).
       d = 3: e 9, σ −5, b₂ 7, (1, 6), odd: ⟨1⟩ ⊕ 6⟨−1⟩ = CP² # 6CP̄².
       d = 4: e 24, σ −16, b₂ 22, (3, 19), even: 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H (K3).
       d = 5: e 55, σ −35, b₂ 53, (9, 44), odd: 9⟨1⟩ ⊕ 44⟨−1⟩.
    (Check d = 4: −d(d² − 4)/3 = −4·12/3 = −16; spin and σ ≡ 0 mod 16, as
    Rokhlin requires.) (c) H ⊕ ⟨1⟩ is odd (it contains ⟨1⟩), indefinite,
    rank 3, σ = 1, and so is 2⟨1⟩ ⊕ ⟨−1⟩: isomorphic by the
    classification (in fact the manifolds are diffeomorphic). H is even,
    ⟨1⟩ ⊕ ⟨−1⟩ is odd: not isomorphic, so S² × S² and CP² # CP̄² are not
    even homotopy equivalent (Whitehead-Milnor: π₁ = 1 manifolds are
    homotopy equivalent iff their forms are isomorphic).
    Common mistakes: forgetting "indefinite" in the classification; using
    b₂ = e − 2 when b₁ ≠ 0; assuming every even form has σ ≡ 0 mod 16
    (that is a SMOOTH constraint, Rokhlin).
    Now you try: b±, type of X₆. (b⁺ = 21, b⁻ = 85, even: 8(−E₈) ⊕ 21H)

D10. (a) Using Rokhlin, Donaldson and Furuta, decide which of the forms
    E₈, E₈ ⊕ E₈, E₈ ⊕ H, 2(−E₈) ⊕ H, 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H, ⟨1⟩ ⊕ 9⟨−1⟩ are forms
    of closed smooth simply connected 4-manifolds, and which of
    topological ones. (b) State the adjunction inequality and derive a
    lower bound for the genus of a surface in K3 representing a class of
    square 2k > 0; state the Thom conjecture and compute the minimal
    genus of 3h, 4h, 6h in CP². (c) Show that X₅ is homeomorphic but not
    diffeomorphic to 9CP² # 44CP̄².
    Answer: (a) smooth: only 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H (K3) and ⟨1⟩ ⊕ 9⟨−1⟩ (CP² #
    9CP̄²); topological: all six (Freedman). (b) g ≥ k + 1; 1, 3, 10.
    (c) same form, SW(X₅) ≠ 0 = SW(connected sum).
    Teacher explains: (a) FREEDMAN realises every unimodular form by a
    closed simply connected topological 4-manifold. Smoothly: E₈ and E₈ ⊕
    E₈ are definite and not diagonal: excluded by DONALDSON (also E₈ by
    Rokhlin, σ = 8). E₈ ⊕ H is even with σ = 8 ≢ 0 (mod 16): excluded by
    ROKHLIN. 2(−E₈) ⊕ H: σ = −16 is allowed by Rokhlin, but b₂ = 18 <
    (10/8)·16 + 2 = 22, excluded by FURUTA. 2(−E₈) ⊕ 3H: b₂ = 22 ≥ 22,
    realised by K3. ⟨1⟩ ⊕ 9⟨−1⟩ = CP² # 9CP̄² (odd forms are always
    realised by connected sums of ±CP²). (b) ADJUNCTION: X with b⁺ > 1,
    K a basic class, Σ embedded of genus g ≥ 1 with [Σ]² ≥ 0: 2g − 2 ≥
    [Σ]² + |K·Σ|. K3 has K = 0 (SW(K3) ≠ 0 at the trivial class), so 2g −
    2 ≥ 2k, g ≥ k + 1 (and spheres are excluded: a nonzero class of square
    ≥ 0 is not represented by a sphere). A smooth curve with C² = 2k has
    exactly g = k + 1 (2g − 2 = C² + K·C). THOM (Kronheimer-Mrowka): in
    CP², K = −3h, algebraic curves of degree d minimise genus: 2g − 2 = d²
    − 3d, g = (d − 1)(d − 2)/2: d = 3: 1, d = 4: 3, d = 6: 10. (c) By D9,
    Q(X₅) = 9⟨1⟩ ⊕ 44⟨−1⟩ = Q(9CP² # 44CP̄²), both simply connected
    and smooth (Kirby-Siebenmann 0), so FREEDMAN gives a homeomorphism. X₅
    is Kähler with b⁺ = 9 > 1, so SW(X₅) ≠ 0 (the canonical class is
    basic), while a connected sum of two pieces with b⁺ ≥ 1 (CP² and 8CP²
    # 44CP̄²) has vanishing SW. SW is a diffeomorphism invariant: not
    diffeomorphic.
    Common mistakes: applying Donaldson to indefinite forms; forgetting
    that the adjunction inequality needs [Σ]² ≥ 0 (or b⁺ > 1 and g ≥ 1);
    quoting Thom for d = 1, 2 as "genus 0 is impossible" (they are
    spheres, g = 0).
    Now you try: minimal genus of 5h in CP² and of (2, 3) in S² × S²
    (bidegree curves, g = (a − 1)(b − 1)). (6; 2)

D11. (a) For an alternating knot HFK̂ lies on the line m = a + σ/2 and
    its ranks are the absolute values of the coefficients of Δ. Write
    HFK̂ of the right trefoil and of the figure-eight; read off genus,
    fiberedness and τ. (b) Using τ, prove g₄(T(p, q)) = (p − 1)(q − 1)/2
    and find u and g₄ of T(2,5), T(3,4). (c) Which positive surgeries on
    the right trefoil are L-spaces? Show that 4₁ and 5₂ are not L-space
    knots, and give rank HF̂ of S³₊₁(RHT) and S³₋₁(RHT).
    Answer: (a) 3₁: F at (a, m) = (1, 0), (0, −1), (−1, −2); 4₁: F at
    (1, 1), F³ at (0, 0), F at (−1, −1); g = 1, both fibered, τ = 1, 0.
    (b) u = g₄ = 2 and 3. (c) S³_p for all p ≥ 1; ranks 1 and 3.
    Teacher explains: (a) RHT: Δ = t − 1 + t⁻¹, σ = −2, so m = a − 1:
    rank 1 in each Alexander grading a = 1, 0, −1, Maslov 0, −1, −2.
    Euler characteristic: t − 1 + t⁻¹ ✓ (the sign (−1)^m gives −1 at a =
    0). 4₁: Δ = −t + 3 − t⁻¹, σ = 0, m = a: ranks 1, 3, 1, Euler char. −t
    + 3 − t⁻¹ ✓, total rank 5 = det. Genus = top Alexander grading = 1;
    rank in the top grading 1 ⇒ fibered (Ghiggini, Ni). For alternating
    knots τ = −σ/2: τ(3₁) = 1, τ(4₁) = 0. (b) |τ(K)| ≤ g₄(K) ≤ u(K),
    and for torus knots τ = (p − 1)(q − 1)/2 = g(K) ≥ g₄(K). So g₄ = τ =
    g. The unknotting number is squeezed: T(p, q) can be unknotted by
    (p − 1)(q − 1)/2 crossing changes (from the braid), and u ≥ g₄, so u
    = (p − 1)(q − 1)/2 (MILNOR's conjecture). T(2,5): 2 and 2; T(3,4): 3
    and 3. (c) L-SPACE KNOTS have Δ with coefficients ±1 alternating in
    sign after the top one is 1. 4₁ has a coefficient 3, 5₂ has 2: not
    L-space knots. The trefoil is one (S³₊₅(RHT) is a lens space), with g
    = 1: S³_p(RHT) is an L-space iff p ≥ 2g − 1 = 1. So S³₊₁(RHT) =
    −Σ(2,3,5) has HF̂ = F (rank |H₁| = 1). Negative surgeries on a
    nontrivial L-space knot are never L-spaces; S³₋₁(RHT) = Σ(2,3,7) has
    rank HF̂ = 3 > 1.
    Common mistakes: forgetting the sign (−1)^m in the Euler
    characteristic; confusing τ with −τ for the mirror; believing HFK̂
    of non-alternating knots is determined by Δ (it is not: e.g. rank
    is not always det).
    Now you try: HFK̂ of 5₂ (σ = −2, Δ = 2t − 3 + 2t⁻¹): ranks, Maslov
    gradings, τ, fibered? (F² at (1, 0), F³ at (0, −1), F² at (−1, −2);
    τ = 1; not fibered: rank 2 in top grading)

D12. (a) State the Fox-Milnor condition and use it together with the
    signature to decide sliceness of 3₁, 4₁, 6₁, 3₁ # 3₁ (granny) and 3₁
    # mirror(3₁) (square knot). (b) Compute g₄ of the granny knot and of
    T(2,5) # T(2,3). (c) Show the Poincaré sphere Σ(2,3,5) does not bound
    an integral homology 4-ball in three ways: Rokhlin invariant, the
    d-invariant, and the Casson invariant λ(S³_{1/n}(K)) = (n/2)Δ″_K(1).
    Answer: (a) slice: 6₁ and the square knot; not slice: 3₁ (det 3),
    4₁ (det 5), granny (σ = −4). (b) 2 and 3. (c) μ = 1, d = 2, λ = ±1.
    Teacher explains: (a) FOX-MILNOR: if K bounds a slice disc D, then
    Δ_K(t) ≐ f(t)f(t⁻¹); at t = −1, det K = |f(−1)|² is a SQUARE. Also
    σ(K) = 0. 3₁: det 3, 4₁: det 5: not squares, not slice. 6₁: Δ = −2t
    + 5 − 2t⁻¹ = (2 − t)(2 − t⁻¹) ✓, and indeed 6₁ is ribbon (a band
    move splits it into a 2-component unlink). Square knot K # −K: always
    slice (spin the arc of K in B⁴; it is ribbon). Granny: Δ = (t − 1 +
    t⁻¹)², det 9 (a square!), but σ = −2 − 2 = −4 ≠ 0: not slice.
    (b) g₄ ≥ |σ|/2: granny |σ|/2 = 2 and g = 1 + 1 = 2, so g₄ = 2. For
    T(2,5) # T(2,3): τ is additive: 2 + 1 = 3 ≤ g₄ ≤ g = 3, so g₄ = 3
    (signature also gives |−4 − 2|/2 = 3). (c) Σ(2,3,5) = ∂P(E₈), the
    negative E₈ plumbing, which is spin (all framings even) with σ = −8.
    ROKHLIN: μ = σ/8 mod 2 = 1 is well defined for homology spheres, and a
    homology ball W would be spin with σ = 0, giving μ = 0: contradiction.
    HEEGAARD FLOER: d is invariant under homology cobordism and d(S³) =
    0; a homology ball bounding Y gives d(Y) = 0, but d(Σ(2,3,5)) = 2
    (from the E₈ inequality c₁² + b₂ ≤ 4d: 0 + 8 ≤ 4d, and equality holds).
    CASSON: Σ(2,3,5) = ±S³₊₁(RHT); Δ = t − 1 + t⁻¹ has Δ″(t) = 2t⁻³, so
    λ = ±(1/2)·2 = ±1, odd, hence μ = λ mod 2 = 1 again. (Casson's λ
    alone is not a homology cobordism invariant; its mod-2 reduction is.)
    Common mistakes: "det is a square ⇒ slice" (granny); applying
    Rokhlin to non-spin W (e.g. CP² minus a ball); forgetting that σ
    of a connected sum adds.
    Now you try: g₄ of T(2,7) # mirror(T(2,3)). (τ = 3 − 1 = 2 ≤ g₄;
    two crossing changes turn T(2,7) into T(2,3), giving a slice knot, so
    g₄ ≤ 2: g₄ = 2)

PART B — MATHEMATICAL ONLY
--------------------------
M1. Surfaces and mapping classes. On H₁(T²) = Z², a = (1, 0), b = (0, 1),
    a·b = 1, T_a = [[1,1],[0,1]]. Classify A ∈ SL(2, Z) as periodic
    (give the order), reducible or Anosov (give the stretch factor λ).
    a) χ(Σ₃)                         b) χ(Σ₂ with 3 boundary circles)
    c) the closed non-orientable surface with χ = −2
    d) T² # RP² is which N_k?        e) Klein bottle # Klein bottle
    f) genus of the orientable double cover of N_k
    g) the matrix of T_a³            h) T_a(x) for x = (2, 5)
    i) [[1,0],[4,1]]                 j) [[0,1],[−1,0]]
    k) [[−1,0],[0,−1]]               l) [[−1,1],[−1,0]]
    m) [[2,1],[1,1]]                 n) [[5,2],[2,1]]
    o) [[−3,1],[−1,0]]               p) [[−1,−3],[0,−1]]
